I'm very much in my own body while I'm there. I mean, I feel the physical toll of the work, so I definitely know it's me doing it. I'm just... struggling to get through the shift. But no, I don't feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from things around you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.8, F43.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because ever since they changed my warehouse hours and put in this new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. It's affecting everything. I'm missing shifts, I'm skipping out on my church commitments, and I've been snapping at my family a lot. Honestly, some mornings I just sit there in my work boots... wondering if I can even face the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overly worried or anxious about things lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel worried or anxious, no. It's not like I'm sitting around sweating over what's going to happen next or anything like that. It's more just... I don't know, a heavy feeling about having to go in and deal with it all. But no, I wouldn't say I'm overly worried in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm feeling sad or down most of the time. I mean, I'm definitely frustrated and overwhelmed by all the changes at work, but it's not a deep sadness or anything like that. It's more of a struggle to just get through the day with this new system.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the changes were implemented?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, like I said before, I've missed a few shifts entirely because I just couldn't bring myself to go in. But when I'm actually there on the floor? No, I don't avoid any specific tasks. I just... I do what needs to be done. I don't hide from the new inventory system or anything like that, I just struggle to get through it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself go through the motions at work?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I've ever felt like that. I'm very much in my own body while I'm there. I mean, I feel the physical toll of the work, so I definitely know it's me doing it. I'm just... struggling to get through the shift. But no, I don't feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from things around you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb. Like I was just saying, I feel the physical work, and I definitely feel the stress of it all. So, no, I don't feel disconnected from things. I'm very much aware of what's going on around me, it's just... it's just a lot to handle right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions of daily life without any sense of purpose or fulfillment?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel like I'm just going through the motions without purpose. I mean, my purpose is to do my job and pay my bills. It's just that the job itself has gotten so much harder lately with all the changes. I don't feel empty or anything like that, just really overwhelmed and stressed out by the workload.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I still like the things I've always liked. I mean... sometimes I'm just too beat after a shift to actually do much of anything, but the interest is definitely still there. I still enjoy my time off just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not unusually tired. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, sure, but... that's just from the physical work. I'm on my feet a lot, moving boxes and stocking shelves, so being worn out at the end of the day is pretty normal for me. I wouldn't say it's anything out of the ordinary, just the usual physical fatigue from the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that at all. I'm not... walking around in a daze or anything like that. I know exactly where I am and what I'm doing. It's really just the stress of all the new changes at work that's getting to me, but my head is clear. I definitely don't feel disconnected from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience emotions as strongly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel things just as much as I always have. I mean... I still get frustrated, I still laugh at a good joke. My emotions are perfectly normal. Like I said, it's just the stress from work that's really getting to me right now, but it hasn't numbed my feelings or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the changes at work were reversed?\n\nPatient: I imagine they would, yeah. But the thing is, those work changes are still going on right now. I haven't had a real break from the new schedule yet, or... or the fallout from it all. It's just been constant. Honestly, some mornings I just sit there in my work boots... wondering if I can even face the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overly worried or anxious about things lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel worried or anxious, no. It's not like I'm sitting around sweating over what's going to happen next or anything like that. It's more just... I don't know, a heavy feeling about having to go in and deal with it all. But no, I wouldn't say I'm overly worried in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm feeling sad or down most of the time. I mean, I'm definitely frustrated and overwhelmed by all the changes at work, but it's not a deep sadness or anything like that. It's more of a struggle to just get through the day with this new system.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the changes were implemented?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, like I said before, I've missed a few shifts entirely because I just couldn't bring myself to go in. But when I'm actually there on the floor? No, I don't avoid any specific tasks. I just... I do what needs to be done. I don't hide from the new inventory system or anything like that, I just struggle to get through it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself go through the motions at work?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I've ever felt like that. I'm very much in my own body while I'm there. I mean, I feel the physical toll of the work, so I definitely know it's me doing it. I'm just... struggling to get through the shift. But no, I don't feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from things around you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb. Like I was just saying, I feel the physical work, and I definitely feel the stress of it all. So, no, I don't feel disconnected from things. I'm very much aware of what's going on around me, it's just... it's just a lot to handle right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions of daily life without any sense of purpose or fulfillment?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel like I'm just going through the motions without purpose. I mean, my purpose is to do my job and pay my bills. It's just that the job itself has gotten so much harder lately with all the changes. I don't feel empty or anything like that, just really overwhelmed and stressed out by the workload.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I still like the things I've always liked. I mean... sometimes I'm just too beat after a shift to actually do much of anything, but the interest is definitely still there. I still enjoy my time off just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not unusually tired. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, sure, but... that's just from the physical work. I'm on my feet a lot, moving boxes and stocking shelves, so being worn out at the end of the day is pretty normal for me. I wouldn't say it's anything out of the ordinary, just the usual physical fatigue from the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that at all. I'm not... walking around in a daze or anything like that. I know exactly where I am and what I'm doing. It's really just the stress of all the new changes at work that's getting to me, but my head is clear. I definitely don't feel disconnected from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience emotions as strongly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel things just as much as I always have. I mean... I still get frustrated, I still laugh at a good joke. My emotions are perfectly normal. Like I said, it's just the stress from work that's really getting to me right now, but it hasn't numbed my feelings or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the changes at work were reversed?\n\nPatient: I imagine they would, yeah. But the thing is, those work changes are still going on right now. I haven't had a real break from the new schedule yet, or... or the fallout from it all. It's just been constant. So I haven't really had a chance to see what it would feel like if things just went back to how they were.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stressors or changes in your life outside of work recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the work stuff. I mean, my life outside of the warehouse is pretty quiet. I just go to work, come home, maybe watch some TV. Nothing else has really changed or been a problem lately. It's just that new schedule they put me on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms started immediately after the changes at work were implemented, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: It wasn't exactly on day one, but there wasn't much of a delay, no. I'd say I started coming apart within the first few weeks after the warehouse changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system. \n\nBefore that... I was doing fine. I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together without any issues. But yeah, once those changes hit, it only took a couple of weeks for everything to just get to be too much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because ever since they changed my warehouse hours and put in this new inventory system, I just feel like I’m falling apart. It's affecting everything. I'm missing shifts, I'm skipping out on my church commitments, and I've been snapping at my family a lot. Honestly, some mornings I just sit there in my work boots... wondering if I can even face the place anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overly worried or anxious about things lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really say I feel worried or anxious, no. It's not like I'm sitting around sweating over what's going to happen next or anything like that. It's more just... I don't know, a heavy feeling about having to go in and deal with it all. But no, I wouldn't say I'm overly worried in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm feeling sad or down most of the time. I mean, I'm definitely frustrated and overwhelmed by all the changes at work, but it's not a deep sadness or anything like that. It's more of a struggle to just get through the day with this new system.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the changes were implemented?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, like I said before, I've missed a few shifts entirely because I just couldn't bring myself to go in. But when I'm actually there on the floor? No, I don't avoid any specific tasks. I just... I do what needs to be done. I don't hide from the new inventory system or anything like that, I just struggle to get through it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself go through the motions at work?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I've ever felt like that. I'm very much in my own body while I'm there. I mean, I feel the physical toll of the work, so I definitely know it's me doing it. I'm just... struggling to get through the shift. But no, I don't feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from things around you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb. Like I was just saying, I feel the physical work, and I definitely feel the stress of it all. So, no, I don't feel disconnected from things. I'm very much aware of what's going on around me, it's just... it's just a lot to handle right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions of daily life without any sense of purpose or fulfillment?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel like I'm just going through the motions without purpose. I mean, my purpose is to do my job and pay my bills. It's just that the job itself has gotten so much harder lately with all the changes. I don't feel empty or anything like that, just really overwhelmed and stressed out by the workload.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I still like the things I've always liked. I mean... sometimes I'm just too beat after a shift to actually do much of anything, but the interest is definitely still there. I still enjoy my time off just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not unusually tired. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, sure, but... that's just from the physical work. I'm on my feet a lot, moving boxes and stocking shelves, so being worn out at the end of the day is pretty normal for me. I wouldn't say it's anything out of the ordinary, just the usual physical fatigue from the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that at all. I'm not... walking around in a daze or anything like that. I know exactly where I am and what I'm doing. It's really just the stress of all the new changes at work that's getting to me, but my head is clear. I definitely don't feel disconnected from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience emotions as strongly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel things just as much as I always have. I mean... I still get frustrated, I still laugh at a good joke. My emotions are perfectly normal. Like I said, it's just the stress from work that's really getting to me right now, but it hasn't numbed my feelings or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the changes at work were reversed?\n\nPatient: I imagine they would, yeah. But the thing is, those work changes are still going on right now. I haven't had a real break from the new schedule yet, or... or the fallout from it all. It's just been constant. So I haven't really had a chance to see what it would feel like if things just went back to how they were.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stressors or changes in your life outside of work recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the work stuff. I mean, my life outside of the warehouse is pretty quiet. I just go to work, come home, maybe watch some TV. Nothing else has really changed or been a problem lately. It's just that new schedule they put me on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms started immediately after the changes at work were implemented, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: It wasn't exactly on day one, but there wasn't much of a delay, no. I'd say I started coming apart within the first few weeks after the warehouse changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system. \n\nBefore that... I was doing fine. I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together without any issues. But yeah, once those changes hit, it only took a couple of weeks for everything to just get to be too much.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about going to work?\n\nPatient: It's been about five months now. Yeah, right around five months. It's been going on ever since they rolled out that new warehouse schedule and the new inventory system.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding talking or thinking about work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid it. I mean, I don't really go out of my way to talk about it either, since it's just a warehouse job, but... if it comes up, it comes up. I'm not trying to block it out or anything when I'm at home. I just don't like actually going there and dealing with the new setup.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't hide from the new inventory system or anything like that, I just struggle to get through it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself go through the motions at work?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I've ever felt like that. I'm very much in my own body while I'm there. I mean, I feel the physical toll of the work, so I definitely know it's me doing it. I'm just... struggling to get through the shift. But no, I don't feel like I'm watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from things around you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb. Like I was just saying, I feel the physical work, and I definitely feel the stress of it all. So, no, I don't feel disconnected from things. I'm very much aware of what's going on around me, it's just... it's just a lot to handle right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions of daily life without any sense of purpose or fulfillment?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel like I'm just going through the motions without purpose. I mean, my purpose is to do my job and pay my bills. It's just that the job itself has gotten so much harder lately with all the changes. I don't feel empty or anything like that, just really overwhelmed and stressed out by the workload.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I still like the things I've always liked. I mean... sometimes I'm just too beat after a shift to actually do much of anything, but the interest is definitely still there. I still enjoy my time off just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, not unusually tired. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, sure, but... that's just from the physical work. I'm on my feet a lot, moving boxes and stocking shelves, so being worn out at the end of the day is pretty normal for me. I wouldn't say it's anything out of the ordinary, just the usual physical fatigue from the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that at all. I'm not... walking around in a daze or anything like that. I know exactly where I am and what I'm doing. It's really just the stress of all the new changes at work that's getting to me, but my head is clear. I definitely don't feel disconnected from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience emotions as strongly as you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I feel things just as much as I always have. I mean... I still get frustrated, I still laugh at a good joke. My emotions are perfectly normal. Like I said, it's just the stress from work that's really getting to me right now, but it hasn't numbed my feelings or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms would improve if the changes at work were reversed?\n\nPatient: I imagine they would, yeah. But the thing is, those work changes are still going on right now. I haven't had a real break from the new schedule yet, or... or the fallout from it all. It's just been constant. So I haven't really had a chance to see what it would feel like if things just went back to how they were.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stressors or changes in your life outside of work recently?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the work stuff. I mean, my life outside of the warehouse is pretty quiet. I just go to work, come home, maybe watch some TV. Nothing else has really changed or been a problem lately. It's just that new schedule they put me on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms started immediately after the changes at work were implemented, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: It wasn't exactly on day one, but there wasn't much of a delay, no. I'd say I started coming apart within the first few weeks after the warehouse changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system. \n\nBefore that... I was doing fine. I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together without any issues. But yeah, once those changes hit, it only took a couple of weeks for everything to just get to be too much.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about going to work?\n\nPatient: It's been about five months now. Yeah, right around five months. It's been going on ever since they rolled out that new warehouse schedule and the new inventory system.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding talking or thinking about work when you're not there?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid it. I mean, I don't really go out of my way to talk about it either, since it's just a warehouse job, but... if it comes up, it comes up. I'm not trying to block it out or anything when I'm at home. I just don't like actually going there and dealing with the new setup.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant decrease in your work performance since the changes were implemented?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Actually, my supervisor... well, he's documented some problems with my reliability lately. He even talked to me about maybe taking some medical leave. \n\nAnd it's not just at the warehouse, either. It's spilling over into other things. I've had to pull back from my duties at church... things that people really counted on me for. I just haven't been able to keep up with it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think all of your current problems would disappear if the issues at work were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know for sure. I'd like to think so. If they just went back to the old schedule and the way we used to do things, I think... well, I think I'd feel a lot better. But right now, it's hard to say if everything would just go back to normal instantly. I just know that's where all this started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
